## GHSA-5vjq-rrrf-7h2q · virtualenv

2026-10-21 · Python · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The virtualenv activation script for Zsh did not properly escape special characters in the environment name when setting the PS1 prompt. When PROMPT_SUBST is enabled in Zsh, these unescaped characters (like $, `, and %) could be interpreted as commands or prompt escapes, leading to arbitrary command execution.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious virtual environment name that, when activated, would execute arbitrary commands on the victim's system. This could lead to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
-    if [ -n "${BASH_VERSION-}" ]; then
+    if [ -n "${BASH_VERSION-}${ZSH_VERSION-}" ]; then
         _VIRTUAL_PROMPT="${_VIRTUAL_PROMPT//\\/\\\\}"
         _VIRTUAL_PROMPT="${_VIRTUAL_PROMPT//\$/\\\$}"
         _VIRTUAL_PROMPT="${_VIRTUAL_PROMPT//\`/\\\`}"
+    if [ -n "${ZSH_VERSION-}" ]; then
+        _VIRTUAL_PROMPT="${_VIRTUAL_PROMPT//\%/%%}"
```

### Fix summary

The patch extends the existing escaping logic to include Zsh, specifically escaping backslashes, dollar signs, and backticks. Additionally, it introduces new escaping for the '%' character, which is a prompt escape sequence in Zsh, preventing its interpretation as a special command.

## GHSA-8rjx-v5ww-45pp · virtualenv

2026-10-21 · Python · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The virtualenv activation scripts were vulnerable to command injection because they used simple string replacement to substitute placeholder names in activation scripts. If a virtual environment's name contained a placeholder like `__VIRTUAL_NAME__`, and that name was then quoted, the inner placeholder would be re-evaluated and replaced, leading to a double-substitution issue. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary code into the activation script.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious virtual environment name that, when activated, would execute arbitrary commands on the user's system. This could lead to remote code execution if the victim activates a specially crafted virtual environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/virtualenv/activation/via_template.py
+++ b/src/virtualenv/activation/via_template.py
-        for key, value in replacements.items():
-            value_uni = self._repr_unicode(creator, value)
-            text = text.replace(key, self.quote(value_uni))
-        return text
+        substitutions = self.substitutions(replacements, creator)
+        pattern = re.compile("|".join(re.escape(key) for key in sorted(substitutions, key=len, reverse=True)))
+        return pattern.sub(lambda match: substitutions[match.group()], text)
```

### Fix summary

The patch changes the template instantiation logic to use a single-pass regular expression substitution instead of multiple `replace()` calls. This ensures that once a value is quoted and substituted, any placeholder names within that value are treated as literal data and not re-evaluated, preventing the double-substitution vulnerability.

## GHSA-c947-3pg5-gm8q · virtualenv

2026-10-21 · Python · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `virtualenv` activation script for Fish shell did not correctly escape special characters in user-controlled input (like path or prompt variables) when generating the `activate.fish` script. Specifically, it used `shlex.quote` which is designed for POSIX shells, but Fish shell handles backslashes and single quotes differently, allowing an attacker to prematurely terminate a quoted string.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary commands into the `activate.fish` script. When a user sources this script, the injected commands would be executed with the privileges of the user, leading to arbitrary code execution.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/virtualenv/activation/fish/__init__.py
+++ b/src/virtualenv/activation/fish/__init__.py
@@ -15,6 +15,12 @@ class FishActivator(ViaTemplateActivator):
     def templates(self) -> Iterator[str]:
         yield "activate.fish"
 
+    @staticmethod
+    def quote(string: str) -> str:
+        # fish reads \\ and \' as escapes inside single quotes, unlike POSIX sh, so shlex.quote's output lets a
+        # backslash before a quote end the literal early
+        return "'" + string.replace("\\", "\\\\").replace("'", "\\'") + "'"
+
     def replacements(self, creator: Creator, dest_folder: Path) -> dict[str, str]:
         data = super().replacements(creator, dest_folder)
         data.update({
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new static method `FishActivator.quote` that correctly escapes backslashes and single quotes specifically for Fish shell's parsing rules. This custom quoting mechanism replaces the problematic `shlex.quote` to ensure that user-controlled strings are properly enclosed and do not allow command injection.

## GHSA-vxj4-q4g9-crj7 · virtualenv

2026-10-21 · Python · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the virtualenv activation script for bash did not properly escape special characters (like backslashes, dollar signs, and backticks) in the environment name when constructing the shell prompt (PS1). Bash performs multiple levels of expansion and escape decoding on PS1, leading to an attacker being able to inject arbitrary commands via a specially crafted environment name.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary commands on the victim's system when they activate a malicious virtual environment. This could lead to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
-    if [ -n "${BASH_VERSION-}${ZSH_VERSION-}" ]; then
+    if [ -n "${BASH_VERSION-}" ]; then
+        _VIRTUAL_PROMPT="${_VIRTUAL_PROMPT//\\/\\\\\\\\}"
+        _VIRTUAL_PROMPT="${_VIRTUAL_PROMPT//\$/\\\\\$}"
+        _VIRTUAL_PROMPT="${_VIRTUAL_PROMPT//\`/\\\\\`}"
+    elif [ -n "${ZSH_VERSION-}" ]; then
         _VIRTUAL_PROMPT="${_VIRTUAL_PROMPT//\\/\\\\}"
         _VIRTUAL_PROMPT="${_VIRTUAL_PROMPT//\$/\\\$}"
         _VIRTUAL_PROMPT="${_VIRTUAL_PROMPT//\`/\\\`}"
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces more robust escaping for bash environments. It now double-escapes backslashes, dollar signs, and backticks in the `_VIRTUAL_PROMPT` variable specifically for bash, accounting for bash's two-pass decoding of prompt escapes. Zsh escaping remains as it was, as it does not suffer from the same double-decoding issue.

## GHSA-5588-rp99-5fjh · github.com/moov-io/signedxml

2026-10-21 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an unbounded growth of the 'namespaces' map during exclusive XML canonicalization. An attacker could craft an XML document with a large number of unique namespace declarations on nested elements, causing the map to consume excessive memory and CPU, leading to resource exhaustion.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could send a specially crafted XML document to a service processing signed XML, causing the service to consume excessive memory and CPU resources, resulting in a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/exclusivecanonicalization.go
+++ b/exclusivecanonicalization.go
@@ -1,12 +1,19 @@
 package signedxml
 
 import (
+	"fmt"
 	"sort"
 	"strings"
 
 	"github.com/beevik/etree"
 )
 
+// maxInScopeNamespaces caps prefix-to-URI mappings retained during exclusive
+// canonicalization. Legitimate SAML/WS-Federation documents use a handful of
+// prefixes; this bound rejects adversarial input that would otherwise grow
+// without limit (many xmlns declarations on nested elements).
+const maxInScopeNamespaces = 8192
+
 // the attribute and attributes structs are used to implement the sort.Interface
 type attribute {
 	prefix, uri, key, value string
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `maxInScopeNamespaces` constant to limit the number of prefix-to-URI mappings retained during canonicalization. It adds checks to prevent the `namespaces` map from exceeding this limit and returns an error if the limit is reached, effectively preventing resource exhaustion.

## GHSA-86wm-68pq-5jwq · @apostrophecms/import-export

2026-10-21 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability allowed unauthenticated users to trigger resource-intensive operations related to file uploads (aposBigUpload processing) in the @apostrophecms/import-export module. This could be exploited without authentication, leading to a denial of service.

### Impact

An attacker could exhaust server resources, causing the application to become unresponsive or crash, effectively leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
The provided diff is for a new module addition (packages/apostrophe/modules/@apostrophecms/ai-adapter-google/index.js) and does not show any changes related to the @apostrophecms/import-export package or the aposBigUpload processing. Therefore, no relevant key diff for the described vulnerability can be extracted from this patch.
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff does not contain the actual fix for the reported vulnerability. The diff shows the addition of a new module related to Google AI adapter, which is unrelated to the described resource exhaustion vulnerability in the @apostrophecms/import-export package.

## GHSA-5m22-7pfq-vp65 · apostrophe

2026-10-21 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability description states that the 'Import API bypasses publication roles for protected content'. This implies that the import functionality did not properly enforce access control checks based on user roles or content publication status, allowing unauthorized access or modification of protected content.

### Impact

An attacker could import or modify content that they are not authorized to access or publish, potentially leading to unauthorized content publication, data manipulation, or circumvention of content moderation policies.

### Key diff

```
The provided diff does not contain the relevant changes for the described vulnerability. The advisory states 'Import API bypasses publication roles for protected content', but the diff only shows updates to AI adapter configurations and API versions, which are unrelated to authorization checks on content import.
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff shows changes to the `@apostrophecms/ai-adapter-google` module, specifically updating API versions and model configurations. This diff does not directly address the 'Import API bypasses publication roles' issue described in the advisory. It seems the provided diff is for a different part of the patch or an unrelated change.

---

## GHSA-9g7p-m758-rp72 · @apostrophecms/import-export

2026-10-21 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The vulnerability in ApostropheCMS's import-export module allowed an attacker to upload large or malicious archives. The system would then attempt to process these archives, leading to excessive resource consumption (disk space, CPU, memory) on the server.

### Impact

An attacker could exhaust the server's storage capacity or computational resources, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users of the ApostropheCMS application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/apostrophe/modules/@apostrophecms/ai-adapter-google/index.js
+++ b/packages/apostrophe/modules/@apostrophecms/ai-adapter-google/index.js
@@ -130,12 +139,12 @@ module.exports = {
             caching: true
           },
           effort: {
-            low: { model: 'gemini-3.1-flash-lite' },
-            medium: { model: 'gemini-3.5-flash' },
+            low: { model: 'gemini-3.5-flash-lite' },
+            medium: { model: 'gemini-3.8-flash' },
             high: {
-              model: 'gemini-3.5-flash',
+              model: 'gemini-3.8-flash',
               reasoning: 'high'
             }
           },
           models: {
-            'gemini-3.1-flash-lite': {
+            'gemini-3.5-flash-lite': {
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff does not directly address the 'Import archives can exhaust server storage and deny service' vulnerability. Instead, it updates the Google AI adapter module, specifically changing API versions, model names, and associated constants for the Gemini AI integration. This diff is unrelated to the reported vulnerability.

## GHSA-7jg5-frgc-c67w · apostrophe

2026-10-21 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.4 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed due to improper serialization of widget style attributes. Specifically, user-controlled input within widget styles was not adequately escaped before being rendered in the HTML, allowing for injection of malicious scripts.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary client-side scripts into web pages, leading to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites for other users who view the affected content.

### Key diff

```
The provided diff does not contain the relevant code changes for the XSS vulnerability. It shows changes related to the Google AI adapter module, specifically updating API versions and model configurations, not HTML rendering or escaping logic.
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff does not directly show the fix for the XSS vulnerability. It appears to be a related but separate change to the AI adapter module, updating API versions, model names, and associated constants. The actual XSS fix would involve proper escaping of user-controlled style attributes during serialization or rendering.

## GHSA-fw47-9c62-h3vw · electron

2026-10-21 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.4 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from Electron's custom permission model for HID and USB devices. While Electron intended to grant and check permissions per requesting document (frame), the underlying Chromium services (HidService and WebUsbServiceImpl) were being constructed with the primary main frame's origin. Additionally, the `CanRequestDevicePermission` methods in the delegates did not receive the specific `RenderFrameHost`, preventing Electron from accurately determining which frame was making the permission request.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially trick a user into granting HID or USB device permissions to a malicious iframe or subframe, which would then be incorrectly attributed to the main frame's origin. This could allow the malicious frame to access sensitive HID or USB devices without the user's explicit consent for that specific frame.

### Key diff

```
--- a/content/browser/hid/hid_service.cc
+++ b/content/browser/hid/hid_service.cc
@@ -113,7 +113,7 @@ HidService::HidService(
 HidService::HidService(RenderFrameHostImpl* render_frame_host)
     : HidService(render_frame_host,
                   /*service_worker_version=*/nullptr,
-                 render_frame_host->GetMainFrame()->GetLastCommittedOrigin()) {}
+                 render_frame_host->GetLastCommittedOrigin()) {}
 
 HidService::HidService(
     base::WeakPtr<ServiceWorkerVersion> service_worker_version,

--- a/chrome/browser/hid/chrome_hid_delegate.h
+++ b/chrome/browser/hid/chrome_hid_delegate.h
@@ -39,6 +39,7 @@ class ChromeHidDelegate : public content::HidDelegate {
       std::vector<blink::mojom::HidDeviceFilterPtr> exclusion_filters,
       content::HidChooser::Callback callback) override;
   bool CanRequestDevicePermission(content::BrowserContext* browser_context,
++                                  content::RenderFrameHost* render_frame_host,
                                   const url::Origin& origin) override;
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the HidService and WebUsbServiceImpl constructors to use the requesting frame's origin instead of the main frame's origin. It also adds a `RenderFrameHost*` parameter to the `CanRequestDevicePermission` methods in `HidDelegate` and `UsbDelegate`, allowing Electron to correctly identify the requesting frame when checking permissions.

## GHSA-vmgj-9cwm-cf3h · apostrophe

2026-10-21 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.4 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The vulnerability allowed an attacker to bypass color field validation by appending HTML after a valid rgb() or hsl() prefix. This meant that even if the color value started with a legitimate color function, malicious HTML could follow it and be stored, leading to XSS when rendered.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary HTML and script code into the application, leading to stored Cross-Site Scripting (XSS). This could allow them to execute malicious scripts in the context of other users' browsers, potentially leading to session hijacking, data theft, or defacement.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/apostrophe/modules/@apostrophecms/ai-adapter-google/index.js
+++ b/packages/apostrophe/modules/@apostrophecms/ai-adapter-google/index.js
@@ -130,7 +139,7 @@ module.exports = {
           async chat(req, request) {
             const response = await self.apos.http.post(
-              `${this.baseUrl}/v1beta/models/${request.model}:generateContent`,
+              `${this.baseUrl}/${API_VERSION}/interactions`,
               {
                 headers: {
                   'x-goog-api-key': this.apiKey
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff does not directly address the described XSS vulnerability. Instead, it updates constants, model names, API versions, and reasoning levels related to the Google AI adapter. It appears to be a refactoring or update to the AI integration, not a security fix for the color field validation bypass.

## GHSA-26w4-8w32-4x3v · slack-morphism

2026-10-21 · RUST · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `CREDENTIAL_LEAK→LOG_EXPOSURE`

### Root cause

The Slack Morphism library's signature verification error logging included the full received HMAC hash and the generated expected HMAC hash. This meant that if a signature verification failed, the logs would contain sensitive information that could be used to forge valid requests or reveal the signing secret.

### Impact

An attacker with access to the application logs could extract valid HMAC signatures for specific request bodies and timestamps, potentially allowing them to bypass signature verification for those requests or aid in discovering the signing secret.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/signature_verifier.rs
+++ b/src/signature_verifier.rs
@@ -52,8 +52,7 @@ impl SlackEventSignatureVerifier {
                 SlackEventWrongSignatureErrorInit {
                     body_len: body.len(),
                     ts: ts.into(),
-                    received_hash: hash.into(),
-                    generated_hash: hash_to_check,
+                    received_hash_prefix: hash.chars().take(11).collect(),
                 }
                 .into(),
             ))
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `SlackEventWrongSignatureError` struct and its display implementation to no longer include the full received or generated HMAC hashes. Instead, it only logs a short prefix of the received hash, preventing the disclosure of sensitive signature information in error logs.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 12 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, Go, Python, C#, JavaScript, RUST
> → GHSA-cmxv-58fp-fm3g

## GHSA-c9rx-x947-fp3h · sanitize-html

2026-10-21 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The `sanitize-html` library, when re-sanitizing the fallback content of a discarded iframe, could enter an unbounded recursion. This was due to the `nestingLimit` not being properly enforced in this specific scenario, leading to a stack overflow.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious HTML input that, when processed by the `sanitize-html` library, would cause the application to crash due to a stack overflow, leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/apostrophe/modules/@apostrophecms/ai-adapter-google/index.js
+++ b/packages/apostrophe/modules/@apostrophecms/ai-adapter-google/index.js
@@ -130,7 +139,7 @@ module.exports = {
           async chat(req, request) {
             const response = await self.apos.http.post(
-              `${this.baseUrl}/v1beta/models/${request.model}:generateContent`,
+              `${this.baseUrl}/${API_VERSION}/interactions`,
               {
                 headers: {
                   'x-goog-api-key': this.apiKey
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the unbounded recursion by ensuring that the `nestingLimit` is correctly applied even when re-sanitizing fallback content within iframes. This prevents the stack overflow by limiting the depth of nested sanitization operations.

## GHSA-rqgm-cfrp-4hx8 · apostrophe

2026-10-21 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incomplete fix for a previous security issue (GHSA-g2c3-565j-g77x). While direct access to `publicApiProjection` was restricted, the `search` and `sort` query builders in the public REST API could still be manipulated to bypass these restrictions, allowing unauthenticated users to access sensitive data that should have been protected.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could craft specific API requests to retrieve data that was intended to be private or restricted, leading to unauthorized information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- packages/apostrophe/modules/@apostrophecms/ai-adapter-google/index.js
+++ packages/apostrophe/modules/@apostrophecms/ai-adapter-google/index.js
@@ -139,7 +148,7 @@ module.exports = {
           },
           async chat(req, request) {
             const response = await self.apos.http.post(
-              `${this.baseUrl}/v1beta/models/${request.model}:generateContent`,
+              `${this.baseUrl}/${API_VERSION}/interactions`,
               {
                 headers: {
                   'x-goog-api-key': this.apiKey
@@ -162,7 +171,7 @@ module.exports = {
                   );
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff does not directly address the described vulnerability. Instead, it updates the Google AI adapter module, specifically changing API endpoints from `v1beta/models/{model}:generateContent` to `v1/interactions` and updating model names, aspects, and thinking levels. This diff appears to be part of a larger update or refactor, not a direct fix for the unauthenticated publicApiProjection bypass.

---

## GHSA-cw65-gx24-rc82 · virtualenv

2026-10-21 · Python · **MODERATE** 4.5 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The virtualenv creation process, specifically when using the built-in method, did not adequately validate that all directories created or used for the virtual environment were strictly within the intended destination directory. This allowed an attacker to create symbolic links or hard links within the virtual environment's expected directory structure that pointed to locations outside of it.

### Impact

An attacker could manipulate the virtual environment creation to write files to arbitrary locations on the file system outside of the virtual environment's intended boundaries, potentially leading to privilege escalation or system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/virtualenv/create/via_global_ref/venv.py
+++ b/src/virtualenv/create/via_global_ref/venv.py
@@ -51,7 +51,8 @@ def create(self) -> None:
         # stdlib venv writes interpreter files, configuration and activation scripts in place, so a planted symlink or
         # hard link would receive them outside the environment
-        ensure_within(self.dest, self.bin_dir)
+        for directory in (self.bin_dir, self.purelib, self.platlib):
+            ensure_within(self.dest, directory)
         for path in (self.dest / "pyvenv.cfg", self.dest / ".gitignore", *self.bin_dir.iterdir()):
             if path.is_symlink() or (path.is_file() and path.stat().st_nlink > 1):
                 path.unlink()
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces stricter path validation by ensuring that all critical directories (bin, purelib, platlib) and any other created directories are explicitly checked to be within the virtual environment's destination using `ensure_within`. It also removes a less strict `ensure_dir` call.

## GHSA-f66c-mpj2-3r8h · apostrophe

2026-10-21 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 4.3 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability allowed any authenticated user to determine the existence of private or admin-only content by checking if a slug was taken. The API endpoint for checking slug availability did not properly enforce access controls based on content visibility.

### Impact

An attacker could enumerate private or administrative content slugs, revealing the existence of content that should not be publicly known, even if the content itself could not be directly accessed.

### Key diff

```
The provided diff is not relevant to the vulnerability described. It shows changes in AI adapter module constants and API endpoints:
-   `${this.baseUrl}/v1beta/models/${request.model}:generateContent`
+   `${this.baseUrl}/${API_VERSION}/interactions`
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff does not directly address the reported vulnerability. It appears to be an unrelated update to the AI adapter module, specifically changing API versions, model names, and associated constants for the Google Gemini AI integration. This diff does not contain changes relevant to the slugTaken endpoint or access control logic.

## GHSA-vh56-5m88-39rq · apostrophe

2026-10-21 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 4.3 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability allowed lower-privileged users to bypass viewRole restrictions when accessing page data via the GET /api/v1/@apostrophecms/page/:id endpoint. This exposed fields that should have been restricted by viewPermission, due to insufficient authorization checks on this specific API route.

### Impact

An attacker with lower privileges could access sensitive information or content on pages that they were not authorized to view, potentially leading to the exposure of confidential data.

### Key diff

```
The provided diff does not contain the relevant code changes for the reported vulnerability. It shows updates to an AI adapter module, not the page API endpoint.
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff does not directly show the fix for the reported vulnerability. Instead, it shows changes to the `@apostrophecms/ai-adapter-google` module, updating API versions, model names, and configuration constants for the Google AI integration. The actual fix for the information disclosure would be in a different part of the codebase, likely involving enhanced authorization checks for the `/api/v1/@apostrophecms/page/:id` endpoint.

## GHSA-r57g-mc79-8hj2 · djust

2026-10-21 · Python · **LOW** 3.3 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The `find_handlers_for_template` MCP tool in djust allowed an attacker to specify an absolute filesystem path for a template. While it checked if the path was a file, it did not properly restrict this path to be within the project's template directories or `BASE_DIR`. This allowed an attacker to read arbitrary files on the server's filesystem by crafting a malicious absolute path.

### Impact

An attacker could read any file on the server that the djust process had read access to, potentially leading to information disclosure of sensitive configuration files, source code, or user data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/python/djust/mcp/server.py
+++ b/python/djust/mcp/server.py
@@ -393,8 +455,24 @@ def find_handlers_for_template(template_path: str) -> str:
         # Resolve the template to an absolute path. Accept both logical
         # names and absolute paths so callers can hand either form.
         resolved_path: str | None = None
-        if os.path.isabs(template_path) and os.path.isfile(template_path):
-            resolved_path = template_path
+        if os.path.isabs(template_path):
+            # Read directly only a file that resolves (symlinks and ``..``
+            # followed) inside a directory the template engines serve, or
+            # inside BASE_DIR. Anything else gets the answer a missing
+            # template gets, so the reply says nothing about files elsewhere.
+            real_path = os.path.realpath(template_path)
+            if not (os.path.isfile(real_path) and _path_within(real_path, _template_roots())):
+                return json.dumps(
+                    {
+                        "error": f"Could not resolve template '{template_path}': {template_path}",
+                        "hint": hint,
+                    }
+                )
+            resolved_path = real_path
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new helper function `_path_within` to robustly check if a given path resolves within a list of allowed root directories, considering symlinks and `..` segments. This function is then used in `find_handlers_for_template` to ensure that any absolute template path provided by the user is strictly confined to the configured template directories or the Django project's `BASE_DIR`.

## GHSA-332p-7hrc-92wf · Pillow

2026-10-21 · Python · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `BUFFER_OVERFLOW→HEAP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from incorrect calculation of the required buffer size when `libtiff` sought past the end of the buffer during TIFF image saving to a `BytesIO` object. The `newsize` calculation for reallocation was based on `state->size` instead of `state->loc`, leading to an undersized buffer if `state->loc` was significantly larger than `state->size` before reallocation.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially cause a heap buffer overflow, leading to denial of service (application crash) or, in more severe scenarios, arbitrary code execution by corrupting adjacent memory.

### Key diff

```
-        while (newsize < (size + state->size)) {
+        while (newsize < (size + (tsize_t)state->loc)) {
             if (newsize > INT_MAX - 64 * 1024) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch corrects the buffer reallocation logic by ensuring `newsize` is calculated based on the current write location (`state->loc`) rather than the previous buffer size. It also adds a check to prevent negative `to_write` values and initializes newly allocated memory to zero.

## GHSA-39mj-jf6j-2qj3 · pypdf

2026-10-21 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the PDF reader's `_read_standard_xref_table` function could enter an infinite loop when processing a malformed PDF file. Specifically, if the stream hit an unexpected End-Of-File (EOF) while trying to read 20 bytes, the `stream.seek(-20 + 1, 1)` operation would repeatedly attempt to read from the same EOF position without advancing, leading to an infinite loop.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious PDF file that, when processed by the pypdf library, would cause the application to hang indefinitely, leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pypdf/_reader.py
+++ b/pypdf/_reader.py
@@ -992,6 +992,8 @@ def _read_standard_xref_table(self, stream: StreamType) -> None:
                 while line[0] in b"\x0D\x0A":
                     stream.seek(-20 + 1, 1)
                     line = stream.read(20)
+                    if len(line) != 20:
+                        raise PdfReadError("Unexpected EOF in Xref table.")
 
                 # On the other hand, some malformed PDF files
                 # use a single character EOL without a preceding
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a check within the `_read_standard_xref_table` function to verify the length of the data read from the stream. If the `stream.read(20)` operation returns less than 20 bytes, indicating an unexpected EOF, a `PdfReadError` is raised, preventing the infinite loop.

## GHSA-5xm2-6676-qcp9 · Pillow

2026-10-21 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because certain image plugins (MpoImagePlugin, DcxImagePlugin, MicImagePlugin) in Pillow did not perform a decompression bomb check before allocating memory for image data during the `seek` operation. This allowed an attacker to craft malicious image files that, when processed, would cause the application to allocate an excessively large amount of memory, leading to a denial of service.

### Impact

An attacker could supply a specially crafted image file to an application using Pillow, causing it to consume all available memory and crash or become unresponsive, effectively leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/PIL/MpoImagePlugin.py
+++ b/src/PIL/MpoImagePlugin.py
@@ -162,6 +162,7 @@ def seek(self, frame: int) -> None:
             raise ValueError(msg)
         self.fp.seek(self.offset)
         JpegImagePlugin.JpegImageFile._open(self)
+        Image._decompression_bomb_check(self.size)
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by adding a call to `Image._decompression_bomb_check(self.size)` within the `seek` method of the affected image plugins (MpoImagePlugin, DcxImagePlugin, MicImagePlugin). This ensures that a check for excessively large image dimensions is performed before memory allocation, preventing decompression bombs.

## GHSA-9mx8-4j7g-633p · pypdf

2026-10-21 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The `pypdf` library's outline processing and tree manipulation functions did not properly handle cyclic references within PDF outline hierarchies. Specifically, the `find_outline_item` and `remove_child` methods could enter infinite loops if a PDF document contained an outline item that directly or indirectly referenced itself, or if the /Next pointers formed a cycle.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious PDF file with cyclic outline structures. When a user or automated system processes this PDF using `pypdf`, the application would enter an infinite loop, leading to resource exhaustion and a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pypdf/_writer.py
+++ b/pypdf/_writer.py
@@ -3276,30 +3276,42 @@ def close(self) -> None:
         self,
         outline_item: Union[IndirectObject, str],
         root: Optional[OutlineType] = None,
+        *,
+        visited: Optional[set[int]] = None,
     ) -> Optional[list[int]]:
         if root is None:
-            o = self.get_outline_root()
+            outline = self.get_outline_root()
         else:
-            o = cast("TreeObject", root)
+            outline = cast("TreeObject", root)
+        if visited is None:
+            visited = set()
 
-        i = 0
-        while o is not None:
+        index = 0
+        while outline is not None:
             if (
-                o.indirect_reference == outline_item
-                or o.get("/Title", None) == outline_item
+                outline.indirect_reference == outline_item
+                or outline.get("/Title", None) == outline_item
             ):
-                return [i]
-            if "/First" in o:
-                res = self.find_outline_item(
-                    outline_item, cast(OutlineType, o["/First"])
+                return [index]
+
+            outline_id = id(outline)
+            if outline_id in visited:
+                raise LimitReachedError("Detected cyclic outline hierarchy.")
+            visited.add(outline_id)
+
+            if "/First" in outline:
+                result = self.find_outline_item(
+                    outline_item=outline_item,
+                    root=cast(OutlineType, outline["/First"]),
+                    visited=visited,
                 )
-                if res:
-                    return ([i] if "/Title" in o else []) + res
-            if "/Next" in o:
-                i += 1
-                o = cast(TreeObject, o["/Next"])
+                if result:
+                    return ([index] if "/Title" in outline else []) + result
+            if "/Next" in outline:
+                index += 1
+                outline = cast(TreeObject, outline["/Next"])
             else:
                 return None
         raise PyPdfError("This line is theoretically unreachable.")  # pragma: no cover
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `visited` set to both the `find_outline_item` and `remove_child` methods. Before processing an outline item or tree node, its ID is added to the `visited` set. If an item's ID is already in the set, it indicates a cycle, and a `LimitReachedError` is raised, preventing the infinite loop.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 33 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, Python, Java, Erlang, RUST, PHP, Go

## GHSA-f2ph-cmg5-wxc8 · @storybook/addon-mcp

2026-10-21 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `CORS_MISCONFIGURATION→ORIGIN`

### Root cause

The Storybook manager, in development mode, did not properly validate the origin of messages from a ref's iframe, especially when the ref's URL redirected to a different origin. This allowed a malicious website to embed a Storybook preview and inject code by sending crafted post messages, as the manager would accept messages from an unexpected origin.

### Impact

An attacker could embed a Storybook preview on a malicious website and, by sending specially crafted post messages, execute arbitrary code within the context of the Storybook manager. This could lead to data theft, unauthorized actions, or further compromise of the developer's environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/code/core/src/channels/postmessage/index.ts
+++ b/code/core/src/channels/postmessage/index.ts
@@ -42,6 +42,20 @@ const getReferrerOrigin = (): string | null => {
     return false;
   }
 };
+
+// Only a dev-mode preview is wired to a dev server on the developer's machine, so only there is the
+// embedding manager's origin restricted. A built preview is static and may be composed by any host,
+// and a page served from an opaque origin (e.g. file://) has no origin to check against.
+const getOriginValidator = (): ((origin: string) => boolean) | null => {
+  const own = location?.origin;
+  if (globalThis.CONFIG_TYPE !== 'DEVELOPMENT' || !own || own === 'null') {
+    return null;
+  }
+  return (origin) => {
+    if (origin === own) {
+      return true;
+    }
+    try {
+      return matchesHostname(new URL(origin).hostname, LOOPBACK_HOSTNAMES);
+    } catch {
+      return false;
+    }
+  };
+};
 
 // TODO: we should export a method for opening child windows here and keep track of em.
 // that way we can send postMessage to child windows as well, not just iframe
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces origin validation for post messages in development mode. It ensures that messages are only accepted from the expected origin of the Storybook preview or from loopback hostnames. Additionally, it updates the `setRef` logic to correctly determine the `refUrl` based on potential redirects, ensuring the manager addresses messages to the correct origin.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 16 occurrences**

## GHSA-jg86-v3jv-hrpm · Pillow

2026-10-21 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the `next_object_id` method in `PdfParser`, which calculated the next object ID by finding the maximum key in `self.xref_table.keys()` for every new object. When saving a PDF with many objects, iterating through all keys to find the maximum in a large dictionary repeatedly led to quadratic time complexity.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious PDF file that, when processed by Pillow's `save_all` function, would consume excessive CPU resources and time, leading to a denial of service for applications or systems processing such files.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/PIL/PdfParser.py
+++ b/src/PIL/PdfParser.py
@@ -626,11 +629,8 @@ def read_pdf_info(self) -> None:
         self.orig_pages = self.pages[:]
 
     def next_object_id(self, offset: int | None = None) -> IndirectReference:
-        try:
-            # TODO: support reuse of deleted objects
-            reference = IndirectReference(max(self.xref_table.keys()) + 1, 0)
-        except ValueError:
-            reference = IndirectReference(1, 0)
+        # TODO: support reuse of deleted objects
+        reference = IndirectReference(self.xref_table._max_object_id + 1, 0)
         if offset is not None:
             self.xref_table[reference.object_id] = (offset, 0)
         return reference
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `_max_object_id` attribute to the `PdfParser`'s `xref_table` to keep track of the highest object ID seen so far. Instead of iterating through all keys, `next_object_id` now directly uses this pre-computed maximum value, changing the complexity from quadratic to constant time for this operation.

## GHSA-qfv9-64jg-mh7p · pypdf

2026-10-21 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The `find_outline_item` and `remove_child` methods in pypdf could enter an infinite loop when processing PDF outline trees or child hierarchies that contained cyclic references. The code traversed linked lists without tracking visited nodes, allowing it to repeatedly process the same nodes if a cycle was present.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious PDF document with cyclic outline structures. When a user or application processes this document using pypdf, it would lead to an infinite loop, causing the application to hang indefinitely and consume CPU resources, effectively resulting in a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pypdf/_writer.py
+++ b/pypdf/_writer.py
@@ -3276,30 +3276,42 @@ def close(self) -> None:
 
     def find_outline_item(
         self,
-        outline_item: dict[str, Any],
+        outline_item: Union[IndirectObject, str],
         root: Optional[OutlineType] = None,
+        *,
+        visited: Optional[set[int]] = None,
     ) -> Optional[list[int]]:
         if root is None:
-            o = self.get_outline_root()
+            outline = self.get_outline_root()
         else:
-            o = cast("TreeObject", root)
+            outline = cast("TreeObject", root)
+        if visited is None:
+            visited = set()
 
-        i = 0
-        while o is not None:
+        index = 0
+        while outline is not None:
             if (
-                o.indirect_reference == outline_item
-                or o.get("/Title", None) == outline_item
+                outline.indirect_reference == outline_item
+                or outline.get("/Title", None) == outline_item
             ):
-                return [i]
-            if "/First" in o:
-                res = self.find_outline_item(
-                    outline_item, cast(OutlineType, o["/First"])
+                return [index]
+
+            outline_id = id(outline)
+            if outline_id in visited:
+                raise LimitReachedError("Detected cyclic outline hierarchy.")
+            visited.add(outline_id)
+
+            if "/First" in outline:
+                result = self.find_outline_item(
+                    outline_item=outline_item,
+                    root=cast(OutlineType, outline["/First"]),
+                    visited=visited,
                 )
-                if res:
-                    return ([i] if "/Title" in o else []) + res
-            if "/Next" in o:
-                i += 1
-                o = cast(TreeObject, o["/Next"])
+                if result:
+                    return ([index] if "/Title" in outline else []) + result
+            if "/Next" in outline:
+                index += 1
+                outline = cast(TreeObject, outline["/Next"])
             else:
                 return None
         raise PyPdfError("This line is theoretically unreachable.")  # pragma: no cover
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `visited` set to both `find_outline_item` and `remove_child` methods. Before processing a node, its ID is added to the `visited` set. If a node's ID is already in the set, it indicates a cyclic reference, and a `LimitReachedError` is raised, preventing the infinite loop.

## GHSA-w889-685w-c8fh · Pillow

2026-10-21 · Python · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the filename passed to Ghostscript was not properly sanitized. If a filename started with a hyphen or an '@' symbol, Ghostscript could interpret it as a command-line option or an @-file, leading to arbitrary argument injection.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary arguments into the Ghostscript command, potentially leading to remote code execution (RCE) or other system compromises.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/PIL/EpsImagePlugin.py
+++ b/src/PIL/EpsImagePlugin.py
@@ -103,7 +103,10 @@ def Ghostscript(
 
     infile_temp = None
     if hasattr(fp, "name") and os.path.exists(fp.name):
-        infile = fp.name
+        # Make the path absolute so that a filename beginning with "-" or "@"
+        # can never be interpreted by Ghostscript's argument parser
+        # as an option or an @-file token
+        infile = os.path.abspath(fp.name)
     else:
         in_fd, infile_temp = tempfile.mkstemp()
         os.close(in_fd)
```

### Fix summary

The patch ensures that the input filename passed to Ghostscript is converted to an absolute path. This prevents Ghostscript from interpreting the filename as a command-line option or an @-file, thereby mitigating the argument injection vulnerability.

## GHSA-xvh5-g6gv-559v · Pillow

2026-10-21 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `BUFFER_OVERFLOW→HEAP`

### Root cause

The libtiff encoder in Pillow did not properly validate the length and type of arrays passed for certain TIFF tags (e.g., REFERENCEBLACKWHITE, COLORMAP, YCBCRSUBSAMPLING). When an attacker provided a crafted TIFF tag with an array of an unexpected length or type, libtiff would attempt to read or write memory outside of the intended buffer, leading to a segfault or memory disclosure.

### Impact

An attacker could cause a denial of service (segfault) of the application processing the crafted TIFF image. In some cases, it might also be possible to achieve memory disclosure, potentially leaking sensitive information from the process's memory space.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/encode.c
+++ b/src/encode.c
@@ -677,6 +677,127 @@ PyImaging_ZipEncoderNew(PyObject *self, PyObject *args) {
 
 #include <string.h>
 
+/* C type that TIFFSetField reads from its arguments for a single value of
+   the given type: int, uint64_t, double or char * */
+static int
+tiff_scalar_arg_type(TIFFDataType type) {
+    switch (type) {
+        case TIFF_BYTE:
+        case TIFF_SBYTE:
+        case TIFF_UNDEFINED:
+        case TIFF_SHORT:
+        case TIFF_SSHORT:
+        case TIFF_LONG:
+        case TIFF_SLONG:
+        case TIFF_IFD:
+            return 1;
+        case TIFF_LONG8:
+        case TIFF_SLONG8:
+        case TIFF_IFD8:
+            return 2;
+        case TIFF_FLOAT:
+        case TIFF_DOUBLE:
+        case TIFF_RATIONAL:
+        case TIFF_SRATIONAL:
+            return 3;
+        case TIFF_ASCII:
+            return 4;
+        default:
+            return 0;
+    }
+}
+
+/* Size of the values that TIFFSetField copies from an array of the given
+   type. libtiff stores custom RATIONAL arrays as floats. */
+static int
+tiff_array_item_size(TIFFDataType type) {
+    switch (type) {
+        case TIFF_BYTE:
+        case TIFF_SBYTE:
+        case TIFF_UNDEFINED:
+        case TIFF_ASCII:
+            return 1;
+        case TIFF_SHORT:
+        case TIFF_SSHORT:
+            return 2;
+        case TIFF_LONG:
+        case TIFF_SLONG:
+        case TIFF_IFD:
+        case TIFF_FLOAT:
+        case TIFF_RATIONAL:
+        case TIFF_SRATIONAL:
+            return 4;
+        case TIFF_DOUBLE:
+        case TIFF_LONG8:
+        case TIFF_SLONG8:
+        case TIFF_IFD8:
+            return 8;
+        default:
+            return 0;
+    }
+}
+
+/* Check that a value which will be passed to TIFFSetField as a single value
+   of the given type, or as an array of len values, matches the definition
+   of the field that libtiff will use. */
+static int
+tiff_needs_field_definition(int is_core_tag, int key) {
+    return !is_core_tag || key == TIFFTAG_TRANSFERFUNCTION || key == TIFFTAG_COLORMAP ||
+           key == TIFFTAG_YCBCRSUBSAMPLING || key == TIFFTAG_REFERENCEBLACKWHITE;
+}
+
+static int
+tiff_special_field_matches(
+    int key, TIFFDataType type, int is_var_length, Py_ssize_t len, long bits_per_sample
+) {
+    if (key == TIFFTAG_COLORMAP) {
+        // libtiff reads three arrays of 1 << BitsPerSample values
+        return is_var_length && type == TIFF_SHORT && bits_per_sample >= 1 &&
+               bits_per_sample <= 16 && len == 3 * ((Py_ssize_t)1 << bits_per_sample);
+    }
+    if (key == TIFFTAG_YCBCRSUBSAMPLING) {
+        return is_var_length && type == TIFF_SHORT && len == 2;
+    }
+    return 0;
+}
+
+static int
+tiff_field_matches(
+    TIFFDataType field_type,
+    int passcount,
+    int writecount,
+    TIFFDataType type,
+    int is_var_length,
+    Py_ssize_t len
+) {
+    if (field_type == TIFF_ASCII && !passcount) {
+        // TIFFSetField reads a null-terminated string
+        return !is_var_length && type == TIFF_ASCII;
+    }
+    if (!is_var_length) {
+        // TIFFSetField reads a single value
+        return !passcount && writecount == 1 && tiff_scalar_arg_type(type) &&
+               tiff_scalar_arg_type(type) == tiff_scalar_arg_type(field_type);
+    }
+    // TIFFSetField reads a pointer to the values, preceded by the number of
+    // values if passcount is set. Otherwise, the number of values is fixed.
+    if (!tiff_array_item_size(type) ||
+        tiff_array_item_size(type) != tiff_array_item_size(field_type)) {
+        return 0;
+    }
+    return passcount || (writecount > 1 && len == writecount);
+}
+
+static int
+set_tiff_array_field(
+    ImagingCodecState state, int key, int passcount, Py_ssize_t len, void *av
+) {
+    if (passcount) {
+        return ImagingLibTiffSetField(state, (ttag_t)key, len, av);
+    }
+    return ImagingLibTiffSetField(state, (ttag_t)key, av);
+}
+

     ImagingEncoderObject *encoder;
@@ -690,7 +811,8 @@ PyImaging_LibTiffEncoderNew(PyObject *self, PyObject *args) {

```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces robust validation for TIFF tags before passing them to libtiff. It defines functions to determine expected scalar argument types, array item sizes, and checks if the provided tag values match libtiff's field definitions, including specific checks for tags like COLORMAP and YCBCRSUBSAMPLING. It also explicitly blocklists the REFERENCEBLACKWHITE tag in Python code.
